## Environment exception: captures

`xvfb-run` is absent and `sudo -n true` reports a password requirement. Per
Part 0 item 5, all screenshot capture steps are skipped on this host; no
password request and no substituted screenshots. Capture tooling will still
be delivered. Headless smoke and strict checks remain mandatory. Visual
acceptance cannot be claimed from static checks.

## Native legacy precision and measurement

V7 `Hunger.value` is f32, whereas thirst, sleep, social needs and health are
f64. Preserve the existing component and its exact legacy trajectory. The
fingerprint hashes native `Hunger.value.to_bits()` (4 little-endian bytes),
and native 8-byte bits for the f64 values. Metric aggregation widens hunger
to f64 and sorts agents before summation. No FFI f32 snapshot downcasts are
used for fingerprints.

The remote clone has no Graft CLI/index; the local macOS graph describes an
older checkout. Use bounded source reads on the remote clone rather than
using that stale graph as evidence.

# Sparse berry edge repair

The exact recipe fails for seeds 247, 338, 345, 383, 426, 728, 759 and 773 in the deterministic 1,156-seed geometry audit (0..=1024, u64::MAX, 2^63, 2^63-1 and 128 high hashed seeds). Camp selection itself succeeded for every audited seed. The failures are fewer than 12 Forest/Plain berry candidates or fewer than three centres separated by six tiles.

When and only when the original candidates fail, exchange a local Plain edge tile in camp radius 6..=16 with a distant Forest tile outside radius16. Rank both deterministically by hash salt9 then y,x; while centres are missing require each new edge to be at least six tiles from the current greedy centres. Never exchange building or water tiles. Re-run the original hash-salt8 cluster recipe after every swap. This preserves total Plain/Forest/Hill/Mountain/River counts exactly, elevation bytes, river geometry, camp, buildings, resident positions and resources. It changes the strict moisture-quantile classification only for deficient seeds, an explicit necessary relaxation to satisfy the requested exact bush count.

All eight failing audited seeds recover with 1..7 swaps and retain exact global class counts. Original successful audited seeds perform zero swaps. Evidence: p2-extended-geometry-results.json and p2-repair-geometry-results.json. These are standalone Python algorithm reproductions, not compiled Rust evidence or a proof over all 2^64 seeds. Runtime harness includes all eight regression seeds and passed the Part 2 full gate.

## Generated camp formation

The required tick-2 all-24-members invariant conflicts with the legacy
lexicographically-first resident anchor: movement precedes formation and a
ring-three camp can span more than radius five from its westernmost resident.
For worlds with shelter planning enabled, prepend real registered Hearth
tiles (sorted y,x) to the formation candidates. Preserve all thresholds,
private birth bookkeeping, causal events, and the exact default candidate
path. This narrow Part 2 rule read is necessary before Part 3 behavior wiring;
no settlement state is fabricated by worldgen or GDScript. A focused test
checks both legacy and generated anchors; worldgen invariants verify 24
members by tick two for normal and repaired regression seeds.

`reset_world` emits its notification deferred: synchronous listeners would
reenter snapshot getters while Rust still holds the mutable node borrow.
Reset preserves simulation speed and rebuilds only this node's engine.
Building snapshots contain registered buildings only; queued camp buildings
appear after the first simulation tick, with no invented render-only ids.

## Part 3 scope conflict and tuning stop

Initial seed1 trace identifies all 11 dehydration deaths as normal social Seeking Agent.
No construction or GatherFood state occurs among thirst>=80 samples. A resident
stands on infinite shore water at thirst100 while following a nonreciprocating
peer; normal social seeking has no urgent-need preemption. Legacy pin passes.

and12, so do not add it. Three allowed knob rounds preserve determinism but do
not solve dehydration. Keep round2 under the stated priority order: it passes
non-extinction and has fewer deaths than the other non-extinct configuration.
The new survival assertion remains unchanged and failing; do not re-pin, ignore,
or delete it to make the gate green. Part3 cannot be pushed under the every-push
green rule. Continue Part4 locally and retain all evidence, then report this
explicit conflict and the last green remote boundary. Night-rhythm stretch

### Part 1 — instruments and the v11 skeleton
- All Pass 5 types land in Part 1 as inert substrate so the probe and FFI code are written once: `SocietyRules::meals: Option<MealRule>`, `SocietyRules::firewood: Option<FirewoodRule>`, `AgentRules::preempt_gathering: bool` (None/false in legacy, v8, v9, v10 and — until Parts 2–4 — v11); `TargetKind::GatherWood` (FFI code 7); `components::Cold`; `sim_engine::FireState`; `SimResources::{wood_tiles, wood_source_max, wood_regen_acc, fires}` (all `BTreeMap`/integer, empty or zero without the rule); six `Tallies`. `game_v11()` = `game_v10()` in Part 1.
- `wood_tiles` / `wood_source_max` are `BTreeMap<(u32, u32), u8>` (section 4.1 asks new collections to be ordered), not `HashMap` like the food maps they mirror.
- Deadwood placement (8.1) lands in Part 1: `WorldGenConfig::{deadwood_spots, deadwood_capacity, deadwood_min_radius, deadwood_max_radius}` (0 everywhere but v11: 20, 6, 5, 18), `camp::deadwood` after `populate`, stream `MovementRng::new(seed ^ DEADWOOD_SALT)` with `DEADWOOD_SALT = 0xDEAD_0D00_F1E1_0005` (distinct from `0xA9E7_0000_0000_0001`, `0x5EED_5A66_E0DE_0001`, `BIRTH_RNG_SALT 0xB117_0000_5EED_0001`, `AGING_RNG_SALT 0x4C1F_E5A1_0000_0001` and the `h64` salt 8). The distance is measured from the Hearth building tile (the formation tile under `SettlementAnchor::Hearth`). Forest candidates are shuffled and taken first, then Plain; a spot needs Chebyshev ≥ 2 from every spot already taken; water, buildings and bushes (`food_source_max` and `food_tiles`) excluded. Nothing reads the wood maps until Part 3, so the Part 1 v11 world runs bit-for-bit like v10 (checked: `game_v11_scene(s)` fingerprints after 1,440 ticks equal the v10 constants for seeds 0–3, with 20 spots placed).
- `burn_per_day(s) = burn_per_hour[s] × burn hours`, where burn hours are computed from the configured window (13 for 18:00–07:00), so the formula stays right if the window ever moves; `firewood::wood_target` is identical for every settlement (it depends only on the rule and the season).
- Haul home generalised in `movement::hauls_home(forage, firewood, food, wood, in_forage_window, in_wood_window, distance)`; without the firewood rule it is exactly Pass 4's `haul_due`. The probe's schema-4 `hauling_now` uses it, so under v11 it also counts wood haulers (unchanged meaning: members walking home with a load).
- Tallies `meals_from_store` and `ground_meals` are counted in every scene (display-only, never read by simulation), so v10 has a baseline for X4: store relief meals (`StockpileConsumeSystem`) and resolved `Consuming { Food }` meals.
- Schema 5 adds 15 keys (all never null): the 12 listed in section 6, `cold_member_hours_total` (the cumulative hourly tally, needed for the summary's cold member-hours) and the two work keys. `adult_work_share` and `idle_share` are written on every row (not only clock rows), which keeps the raw row shape uniform. `idle_share` = residents Idle **and not hauling** / all residents; Pass 4's `noon_idle_share` (= `state_idle / alive`) counts haulers as idle, which differed by the hauling share (≈ 0.025 at noon in v10). The Pass 5 baseline `p5-1_v10_120day` reports both. `fire_lit` = 1 when at least one settlement exists and every settlement's fire is lit.
- `evaluate_pass5.py` needs the configured winter burn per day (default 39 = 3 × 13) and the cold window (default 1320,360) as arguments because samples carry the derived wood target, not the rule. X7 uses periodic samples (every 120 ticks: 22:00, 00:00, 02:00, 04:00 fall in the cold window) as `Σ cold_members / Σ settled_agents` per seed over winter cold-window samples. X4's per-seed clause uses each seed's summed store meals / summed meals from day 3.

### Part 2 — meals at the hearth
- `game_v11()` gains `MealRule { 360, 480, 1080, 1200, meal_hunger 25.0, camp_radius 8 }` exactly as section 7.1.
- Camp wait (7.2): computed once before the agent loop, each member's formation tile and whether its settlement's stockpile holds Food ≥ 1 (sorted settlement id, first settlement wins). In the Idle cascade, when the actionable need is Food and the member is within `camp_radius` of a stocked camp, the agent `continue`s (stays Idle; no construction, social, combat, forage, gather or migration arm) unless thirst exceeds the critical level, in which case it seeks water. "Critical" = `AgentRules::critical_preemption` (75.0 in v8–v11; constant `CRITICAL_NEED = 75.0` if a world has none), strictly above, like the existing preemption. The memory-bias eligibility list drops the Hunger arm for a waiting member so a bias flip cannot send it to a bush either.
- Serving (7.3): `StockpileConsumeSystem` uses `camp_radius` as the serving radius and `meal_hunger` (inclusive, like the relief threshold) inside either meal window, `STOCKPILE_RELIEF_HUNGER_THRESHOLD` (50) outside; the double-dip gate (skip `Seeking/Consuming { Food }`) is kept, one unit per member per pass. Without the rule the Pass 4 radius (`relief_radius` or the proximity radius) and threshold are untouched.
- A store with fewer units than waiting members serves members in AgentId order; the rest see an empty store at the next decision tick and walk to a bush, so no member can wait on an empty store.

### Part 3 — firewood, fire and cold
- `game_v11()` gains `FirewoodRule { 600, 1020, wood_radius 18, haul 2, 1 per spot, regen 500‰, base_days 1.5, winter_prep, burn 1080→420, [2, 1, 2, 3], cold 1320→360, 1500‰ }` exactly as 8.3.
- Regrowth (8.2): `ResourceRegenSystem` adds the wood channel after the food channel under the rule only, with its own `wood_regen_acc` (`acc += regen_permille; units = acc / 1000; acc %= 1000`; zero units skip), refilling `wood_source_max` spots toward their ceiling and recreating emptied spots; never writes zero.
- Firewood arm (8.4): after Forage, before Gather; workers only (not Child, not Elder); members only; inside 10:00–17:00; carried wood below the haul load, inventory room, `wood_in_hand < wood_target`; choice minimises `(claims, chebyshev(agent, spot), y, x)` over spots within `wood_radius` of the formation tile with claims below the cap; claims are counted before the loop from `Seeking { GatherWood }` agents with a `SeekTarget` and incremented as assigned. Re-targeting mirrors Pass 4's forage (AgentId order, claims recomputed after the pass, the empty spot excluded, Idle when nothing qualifies). A wood seeker that somehow has no target is re-targeted the same way, never frozen.
- Under the firewood rule a member carrying a full wood **or** food load takes none of the Forage, Firewood or Gather arms and is not assigned to build (Pass 4's builder `loaded` check extended to wood), so it walks home instead; without the rule Pass 4's checks are unchanged.
- Haul home (8.4): `hauls_home` (Part 1). `StockpileDepositSystem` drains Wood as well as Food under the rule; a haul deposit is counted when either was delivered by a member whose last step was a haul step. Without the rule the Food-only path is byte-identical (a zero `remove` is never called).
- `FireSystem` (8.5), priority 147, interval 60, registered last in `register_default_runtime_systems` (`register_fire_systems`); returns at once without the rule. `SimResources::fires` is rebuilt on each run for the settlements that exist (id order). Cold applies with seasons on, season winter (`season_at == 3`) and the minute inside the cold window, to every member of a settlement whose fire is out; markers are inserted/removed in AgentId order (archetype layout stays seed-independent). `cold_member_hours += marked members` per run.
- `HungerDecaySystem` (8.5): without the rule the original loop runs unchanged; with it, a `Cold` agent grows by `growth_rate × cold_hunger_permille as f32 / 1000` (f32, like `Hunger`), others call `Hunger::tick` as before.
- Shelter planning skips registered deadwood spots (`wood_source_max`): the shelter rings (2–5) touch the deadwood band (5–18) at radius 5. The map is empty in v10, so v10 is untouched.
- FFI (8.6): `get_agent_snapshot.carried_woods` (PackedInt32Array); `target_kinds` code 7; `get_settlement_snapshot.wood_stocks` (PackedInt32Array) and `fire_lit` (PackedByteArray, 1 lit); `get_resource_snapshot` rows of kind 3 for every registered deadwood spot (amount 0 while emptied, like v10 bushes); `get_tallies` adds the six Pass 5 tallies. `settlement_rows_to_dict` takes `resources.fires`; the `#[func]` body still forwards to `collect_settlement_snapshot(&self.engine.world, …)` (P12-γ A4 static check). The existing GDScript consumers ignore kind 3 (`building_renderer` and the HUD keep only kind 0), clamp target code 7 in the inspector and colour `seek_kind` 0 grey, so nothing errors before the art run maps them.

### Part 4 — critical needs interrupt gathering
- `game_v11()` sets `AgentRules::preempt_gathering = true`. In the Seeking branch, before anything else, a `Seeking { GatherFood | GatherWood }` agent whose hunger or thirst is ≥ the critical level (`critical_preemption`, 75.0; inclusive as section 9 says) becomes Idle; the SeekTarget post-pass drops its target the same tick (Idle → no goal) and the next decision tick serves the need through the normal cascade (or the camp wait when it stands at a stocked camp); `gather_preempted += 1`. Carried goods stay in the inventory and are hauled home later. `Consuming` pickups are single-tick and are not interrupted.
- The Part 3 commit `f220395b` left two Clippy findings (`type_complexity` on the wood re-target list, `manual_clamp` in the cold hunger arithmetic); fixed in the Part 4 commit with a `WoodRetarget` alias and `clamp(0.0, SATURATION)` (same result for finite values). The green branch is only moved to a commit whose full gate passed.

### Part 5 — measure and tune
- Round 1 used the section 7–9 defaults. Between rounds, candidate knob settings were screened with a throwaway integration test (not committed) over 8–32 seeds and up to 120 days, so each of the three formal rounds tests an informed configuration; the formal rounds remain the evidence. Screening numbers are in METRICS.md.
- Priority follows the X table order: X3 (stagnation) outranks X5 (forage trips every season) and X9 (evening). `meal_hunger` 15 would have passed X5 in every season but caused starvation and dehydration deaths in long runs (X2), so it was rejected. Round 2 takes `hoard_cap_factor` 1.5, `meal_hunger` 30 and `base_days` 2.5 (all within the allowed ranges; the stock-target values now live in `game_v11()` and v10 keeps its own).
- Round 2 kept (`meal_hunger` 30, `base_days` 2.5, `hoard_cap_factor` 1.5): it fixed X3 (stagnation 9 → 4) and kept X2, X4, X6–X8, X10–X12; X5 autumn/winter and the X9 minimum remain red after rounds 1–2 and twelve screened variants; no round 3 because no screened variant improved them without breaking X3. Hypotheses in METRICS.md.
- Part 5 time: 01:30–02:10 KST.

### Part 6 — look at the screen
- Start 02:09:29, capture finished 02:09:58 KST. Same single command as Pass 4 inside the Aqua session, holding the shared window lock: `with_window_lock.sh 1500 launchctl asuser 501 env CAPTURE_DOCS_DIR=docs/revitalize/shots/pass5 GODOT_BIN=… bash -c 'cd … && bash tools/revitalize/capture.sh final'`. The live game ran v11 as committed in `ceb7cd92` (round 2 configuration, before the Part 7 review fixes). Ten 960 × 540 JPEGs and `visual_qa.md` (43 checks, V1–V6 all PASS); no GDScript, scene or shader edits.
- Answers (one sentence each): **noon (`d2_1200_F2`)** — residents are spread across the forest and plain within about ten tiles of the camp (several carrying the yellow load glyph or heading out under the grey "other target" marker that firewood trips currently share, a few going to the river), with only a handful idle at the stockpile and hearth; **evening/night (`d2_2200_F2N`)** — the village has gathered at the lit hearth and its four shelters, with three or four stragglers still walking in from the river or the bushes; **day 6 noon (`d6_1200_F2`)** — the camp still reads as one village around the hearth, stockpile and five shelters, with work spread around it rather than a second cluster. The renderer draws neither deadwood, carried wood nor the fire state, as expected.

### Part 7 — independent review, final verification, report
- Part 7 start: 02:10 KST (review launched while the Part 5 gate and the capture ran).
- Independent review round 1 (built-in `general-purpose` subagent, fresh context, read-only; given `git diff 5cbcbc4f..HEAD -- rust/ tools/revitalize/`, sections 4, 7, 8, 9 and 11 and the round-2 X table). It found determinism and v8/v9/v10/legacy invariance clean. Findings and dispositions:
  1. Medium — under need fallthrough an empty map hides the Food need, so a hungry member at a stocked camp was not held for its meal (section 7.2 keys on `hunger > HUNGER_THRESHOLD`), the Firewood arm could send it out, and once its hunger reached 75 it could flip between preemption and a new trip every tick. **Fixed**: the camp wait now keys on hunger itself; under `preempt_gathering` a member whose hunger or thirst is already critical starts no Forage, Firewood or Gather trip (so it drifts home under the idle tether and is fed at camp). Tests `harness_meal_waits_when_every_bush_is_empty`, `harness_preempted_gatherer_is_not_sent_out_again`. v10 is untouched (both gates need the meal rule or `preempt_gathering`).
  2. Low — a `Seeking { GatherWood }` agent picks up at any deadwood tile it steps on, not only its target, so the per-spot cap limits trips rather than pickups. **Not changed**: it mirrors `GatherFood`, cannot freeze anyone (the claimed gatherer is re-targeted), and checking the target tile would need `SeekTarget` in the main decision query; recorded as a known limitation.
  3. Low — `hauls_home`'s partial-load branches were untested. **Fixed**: table test `hauls_home_generalises_haul_due_to_wood`.
  The fixes change v11 behaviour, so every final v11 number comes from probes run after them.
- Review round 2 (same reviewer, `git show a27589ad`): all three fixes confirmed, legacy/v8/v9/v10 invariance confirmed, no new defect; finding 2's disposition accepted. Noted: a critical member can still be sent away by an assigned build or the social arm, which was already true before this pass.
- Full gate on code head `a27589ad`: green (debug 1,912 / release 1,912 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored; both Clippy variants; forbidden math 0; bridge build; Godot import; strict check; smoke without `SCRIPT ERROR`/`ERROR:`; Python unit tests). Pass 4 had 1,881 each; the 31 added tests are the Pass 5 harnesses (meals 6, firewood 10, preempt 3) and unit tests.
- Final probes on `a27589ad` (back to back, nothing competing): legacy, v8, v9 (10 and 120 days) and v10 (10 and 120 days) reproduce Pass 4's final summaries on every shared value (only `schema_version` differs). The v11 pair on the same head failed X3 by one day (seed 22, 5-day stall after winter) because the review fix shifted its trajectory, so the third tuning round was used: `max_foragers_per_bush` 2 (screened first). The pre-round-3 v11 evidence is kept as `p5-7_review_head_*`.
- Round 3 (`max_foragers_per_bush` 2) kept as the final configuration: X3 back to PASS (stagnation max 3), X9 median 0.590 and minimum 0.333 (still under 0.35), X5 autumn and winter still 0. Its two runs are the final v11 probes (final code `c77ee27c`, evaluated with the final reproduction receipts and same-host v10 timing). Tuning rounds used: 3 of 3.
- Three-strike stops: X5 autumn/winter (rounds 1–3 plus 18 screened variants) and the X9 minimum (rounds 1–3). Hypotheses in METRICS.md round 2.
- Full gate on the final code head `c77ee27c`: green (debug 1,912 / release 1,912, both Clippy variants, forbidden math 0, bridge build, Godot import, strict check, smoke clean, 27 Python tests); log `verification/p5-7/gate_final.txt`. The green branch is fast-forwarded to the final docs head that follows it (docs only after `c77ee27c`).
- `REPORT_PASS5.md` written in Korean (section 15 format). Part 7 end ≈ 03:25 KST. Elapsed working time ≈ 2 h 30 min (00:56 → 03:25); the 10-hour rule never triggered; no context compaction; nothing dropped from the drop order (cold nights and the preemption extension both shipped). Three-strike stops: X5 autumn/winter and the X9 minimum.

Free disk 141 GiB; system `python3` has numpy 2.4.3, so no venv is created; `gh` is authenticated.
- Part 0: "seeds 0..4" is read as the half-open range (seeds 0–3), as Pass 6 did for the v11 fingerprints. Probe binaries are frozen as copies outside the clone (`/Users/rexxa/work/p7bin/`) so a run never picks up later edits.
- Part 1: the severity draw (`firewood::winter_severity`, `year_at_tick`, `night_tick_at`, `severity_level_at`, `winter_severity_at`) lands in Part 1 as pure reads, so the schema-7 key and the FFI key read real rule state; it is inert because v12 (and v13 until Part 2) set no weights. Without weights `winter_severity` returns 1 (normal) for any caller, and `winter_severity_at` returns −1.
- Part 1: the probe's `winter_severity` is read at the system tick (`resources.current_tick`), as the probe's `season`; the FFI's at the completed tick (`engine.current_tick()`), as the FFI's own `season` and `day_index`. Both follow the night booking of `night_season` (from 00:00 to `burn_end_minute` the evening's day, under `night_season_from_evening`).
- Part 1: `wood_retargets` grows by one each time the decision post-pass finds a `Seeking{GatherWood}` member whose `SeekTarget` tile holds no wood (`old = Some(tile)`), whether or not a new spot is then found; counted in every version, display-only. `fire_blocked_settlements` counts settlements whose rain lasts past the next system tick (`engine.current_tick() < fire_blocked_until`).
- Part 1: `evaluate_pass7.py` takes Z2 and Z5 from `evaluate_pass6.evaluate` with Pass 6's thresholds; Y5's and Y8's floors keep Pass 6's reference, the v11 evaluation `p6-1_v11_targets` (autumn noon work 0.458, winter food 253.5). Z4's floor is 0.9 × the median winter wood gathered of the v12 evaluation (`p7-1_v12_targets`, every v12 winter; v12 has no severity). Z1 adds a `v12_repro` receipt; Z6 and Z7 are receipts; Z8 compares with same-host game_v12 summaries.
- Part 1: `gate_pass7.sh` = `gate_pass6.sh` + `python3 -m unittest discover -s tools/worldmodel -p 'test_*.py'`; until Part 4 creates `tools/worldmodel` tests the step records a skip in `gate.txt` (an empty discovery exits 5 on this Python).
- Part 2: one helper, `firewood::winter_burn_per_hour(resources, rule, tick)`, gives the burn per hour of the season holding `tick` (the severity's rate in a winter with weights, `burn_per_hour[season]` otherwise). `FireSystem` passes the night's booked tick (`night_tick_at`, the evening's), `wood_target` the current tick, and urgency the night's booked tick through `tonight_burn` (= `burn_per_day(night_season)` without weights; unit-tested). The autumn preparation term keeps `burn_per_hour[3]`.
- Part 2: the winter spread regrowth reads the severity of the current tick's winter (`severity_level_at` at `current_tick`, as the Pass 6 regrowth reads its season) and passes `winter_severity_regen_units_per_day[level]` as its per-day value to the unchanged accumulator.
- Part 2: under `claims_bounded_by_amount` an arrived gatherer (`Consuming{GatherWood}`) claims the tile it stands on (where its pickup happens) in both the Firewood arm's pre-pass and the post-pass re-targeting claims; its leftover `SeekTarget` is dropped as for every non-seeking state. `choose_spot` admits a spot while `claims < min(cap, amount)`, `cap` being the per-spot cap after the urgent doubling.
- Part 2: the explicit literals of `harness_winter_severity_table` (u and level for seeds 0–3, years 0–1, weights 300/400/300) were computed by an independent Python re-implementation of the section 7.2 formula, not by the Rust code under test. The same script gives the probe's 128 seed-winters under 300/400/300: mild 41, normal 54, harsh 33.
- Part 3: kind names, `intervention_from_kind`, `home_settlement` and `apply_intervention` live in `sim_systems::runtime::intervention`; schedules (`sim_test::worldmodel::schedule`) and the FFI (`parse_intervention`, `apply_intervention_command`) share them. A schedule row whose "home" names no settlement (e.g. tick 0, before the first settlement forms) applies to settlement id 0, which is never issued, so it is logged with `applied` 0. The FFI's `ok` is false for an unknown kind, a negative amount, or a settlement kind with no settlement (`settlement_id < 0` with no settlement, or an id beyond `u32`); `cold_snap` and `windfall` ignore the settlement and never fail on it; an amount above `u32::MAX` saturates.
- Part 3: the cold-snap flag is updated at the top of every `FireSystem` run (before the burn): set inside the burn window when nights remain, cleared (one night counted off) at the first run outside it. The +1 per hour applies to every settlement while the flag is set; the cold check becomes `(winter or cold snap) and inside the cold window`, so without seasons a cold snap also chills an unlit hearth. Rain is checked per settlement as `current_tick < fire_blocked_until[sid]` (system tick); a raining fire is out and withdraws nothing.
- Part 3: `state_digest` re-implements FNV-1a in `worldmodel/digest.rs` (`probe/fingerprint.rs` must not change) and writes a length before every collection so two different states cannot concatenate to the same byte stream. The prefix-identity test applies each kind at 18:00 of the first day (completed tick 600) on seed 4, where all seven apply a positive amount, and compares digests every 60 ticks for a day after.
- Part 2 gate and probes: gates run in a separate git worktree of the committed head (`/Users/rexxa/work/p7-gate`, detached), so later edits in the clone cannot leak into a gated build; the gate logs go to the clone's `docs/revitalize/verification/p7-<N>/`. The first in-place gate attempt was stopped after its first step for that reason.
- Part 4: the recorder reads the probe's own computations, unchanged: `probe::metrics::sample` (made `pub(crate)`) for the counts and tallies, and `probe::pass5::work_counts` for the numerator of `adult_work_share` — the counting loop of `pass5::metrics` was moved, verbatim, into `work_counts` (and `member_homes`), which `metrics` now calls. A v12 10-day guard with the Part 4 probe (`p7-4_v12_guard_10day`) checks that nothing changed.
- Part 4: row values use the completed tick (`engine.current_tick()`), as the FFI's time snapshot does: `minute_of_day`, `season`, `day_of_season`, `winter_severity` (with the night booking), `year`; `wood_target` is the firewood helper as the systems see it. `settlements_under_rain` equals the probe's `fire_blocked_settlements`. Agents' `age_days` and `lifespan_days` are fractional (ticks / 1440). The agent row joins `collect_agent_snapshot` and `collect_agent_colds` positionally (the same Agent + Position iteration the FFI pairs them by) and keys the result by `agent_id`.
- Part 4: `resources.npy` leaves out tiles whose live value is the infinite sentinel; absent tiles are NaN with `present` 0. Building keys take the kind of the first row a building appears in. The recorder accepts 1–400 days (OOD_long needs 240); the probe keeps its 150-day limit.

Section 7.4 says to soften harsh first: screens A–D (harsh burn 3; harsh share 20 %; harsh fall 5; 40/40/20) did not fix seed 22 and two of them broke replacement (−4) or Z3. In B and D seed 22 has four normal winters (Pass 6's) and still fails, so the cause is the claims fix (section 7.3), which no knob reaches. What does reach it is the mild weight: seed 22's draws are 752 / 423 / 785 / 508, so a mild weight above 508 turns two of its winters mild. Screens E–H span the mild weight 520–600; Z3's no-dark-night share (≤ 0.60) caps it, and 520/280/200 (screen H) passes Z2–Z5. Round 2 adopts it. `cold_hunger_permille` stays 1500 (it may only move once Z2 holds, and nothing asks for it). The harsh weight 20 % is outside the "soften harsh first" letter only in that the harsh share falls rather than its burn; burns and falls keep their start values.

If a later change touches `game_v13()` or the recorder, the dataset is re-recorded (Part 7 says which head it was recorded on).
- Part 6 (bench): the ridge predicts the hourly change of V (v[t+1] − v[t]) from lags 1, 2, 3, 6, 12, 24 where lag 1 is row t itself (so lag 24 is 23 hours earlier), plus hour (24), season (5, including −1) and severity (4, including −1) one-hots; features are standardized on the training rows and the intercept is not penalised. λ is chosen by the mean nMAE over V on A val (action-aware: A val + B val). The action-aware variant adds, per kind, the amounts applied at rows t−23 … t (applied after their row, so an intervention of tick t is a feature of row t and first shows in row t+1). Fire lit is the maximum over settlements, stocks the sums; before the first settlement forms all three are 0.
- Part 6 (bench): rollouts take hour and season from the calendar and hold severity at the start row's value (a model cannot know a future winter's draw); B test rollouts see the recorded interventions as inputs. At whole-day horizons the daily-naive prediction equals persistence (both read the start row's hour); both are reported. T3's rule uses the settlement wood stock at 18:00 against tonight's burn = `winter_severity_burn_per_hour[level] × 13` (`--burn` passes `game_v13()`'s values); the night index is the evening's day of the winter (1–7). T4's cold member-hours run from day 22 18:00 to day 29 07:00 (one-based), covering the seven first-winter nights.
- Part 6: T3 is nearly arithmetic in this simulation (the fire burns only stockpile wood from 18:00, so the 02:00 state follows from the 18:00 stock and the night's rate); the logistic baseline's AUC 1.000 is that arithmetic, not leakage (its inputs are all read at 18:00). The task is kept as specified and the finding is written into BENCH_v0.md with a pointer to a longer-lead task for v1.
- Part 6: the DGX was reachable; the dataset was copied with `rsync` (without `timing.json`) and every manifest hash verified there. No DGX compute was used.
- Part 7 independent review round 1 (built-in `general-purpose` subagent, fresh context, read-only, no builds; given `git diff fe60de21..9775e6b2 -- rust/ tools/revitalize/ tools/worldmodel/`, sections 4, 7, 8, 9, 11 and 12, the round 2 Z table, Z6, BENCH and DATASET). A first reviewer launched in the background went idle without delivering a report; a second one, run with an instruction to also write its report to a file, completed the round. No High or Medium finding; determinism, v8–v12 invariance, the severity draw (recomputed independently against all 128 recorded severities), claims, interventions, recorder fields, splits and metrics clean. Five Low findings:
  1. L1 — `--resume` kept any episode whose hashes verified, even one recorded with other arguments. **Fixed**: `episode_matches` also requires scene, seed, days, every, agent flag, schedule and (when set) git sha; test `resume_keeps_only_the_requested_episode`. Dataset unaffected (all 400 episodes carry `0c46f69c`, recorded in one run).
  2. L2 — `settlements.npy` `wood_target` is the systems' last value (system tick = row tick − 1), so at a season-boundary row it lags the calendar by one row. **Documented** in README (changing it would change the recorded files and the manifest; the FFI's `wood_targets` has the same convention).
  3. L3 — the FFI returned `ok: true` for an explicit settlement id that does not exist. **Fixed**: `ok` is false when a settlement kind names a missing settlement; unit test extended.
  4. L4 — the bench's action features dropped interventions that applied 0 but used the requested amount otherwise. **Fixed**: every intervention is encoded by its requested amount (section 12.2: one-hot × amount); bench re-run.
  5. L5 — the nMAE denominator used every row of the test episodes, including the 23 unscored first rows. **Fixed**: the std is taken over the scored T1 target rows (rows 24 … T−1), T2 uses the same; bench re-run (sanity checks unchanged; T1 A-test mean nMAE ridge 0.099 → 0.100, persistence 0.157 → 0.158).
  None of the fixes touches simulation code or `game_v13()`; the final probes (`p7-7_final_*`, round 2) and the dataset stand.
- Review round 2 (fresh `general-purpose` subagent, read-only, `git show 6df3a7e0`): L1, L3, L4 and L5 confirmed fixed and complete; the L2 README statement confirmed true and the "documented, not changed" disposition accepted; no new code defect. One Low docs finding — METRICS quoted the pre-fix bench numbers — **fixed** (the first-run line is marked superseded and the re-run numbers are written below it). Nits **not changed** (cosmetic, and the final gate was already running on `6df3a7e0`): two comments in `bench_v0.py` (`Ep.actions`, `actions_window`) still say "applied" for the requested amounts, and the `make_dataset.py` docstring still describes `--resume` as a hash check. Noted: with `export.sh` always exporting `WORLDSIM_GIT_SHA`, `--resume` now resumes only within one commit. Review rounds used: 2 of 2.
- Late report from the first round-1 reviewer (the background one that had gone idle; it delivered at 15:11 KST, after the replacement reviewer's rounds). Its five findings: `--resume` not matching the request (rated Medium there; = L1, fixed in `6df3a7e0`), the `wood_target` one-tick lag (= L2, documented), the FFI `ok` for a missing explicit settlement (= L3, fixed), the nMAE denominator (= L5, fixed), and one new Low: `evaluate_pass7.py` keeps `DEFAULT_WEIGHTS = (300, 400, 300)` (the start values) while `game_v13()` uses 520/280/200. **Not changed** (review rounds exhausted and the final gate already green on `6df3a7e0`): every committed Z evaluation (`p7-5_round2_targets`, `p7-7_final_targets`) passes `--severity-permille 520,280,200` explicitly, so no recorded verdict is affected; a run without the flag would test Z3's severity shares against the wrong weights. Recorded as a limitation. That reviewer also rebuilt the sample (byte-identical), ran `test_export_determinism.py` (3/3) and recomputed all 128 round-2 severities (all match).

- Part 1: `sim_core::rng` holds the primitive: `keyed_u64 = m(m(m(m(m(seed ^ stream) ^ subject) ^ tick) ^ slot) ^ replicate)` with `m` one splitmix64 output (`mix`, tested equal to `MovementRng::new(x).next_u64()` and to Vigna's reference `mix(0) = 0xE220A8397B1DCDAF`). `m` is a bijection, so any single-field change changes the value. No Philox: the property required is that a draw depends on its key alone, which a stateless chain gives by construction. `keyed_below(n)` is multiply-shift (`(u × n) >> 64`), one draw, no rejection. Streams: `STREAM_MOVE_PAUSE`, `STREAM_MOVE_DX`, `STREAM_MOVE_DY`, `STREAM_LIFESPAN`, `STREAM_FOUNDER`, `STREAM_NEWBORN` (ASCII constants, distinctness tested). `NoiseFork { tick, replicate }` lives in `sim_core::rng` (re-exported by `sim_engine`); `SimResources::noise_fork` (default `None`) and `noise_replicate_for(tick)`; `apply_noise_fork(engine, replicate)` (free function and method) sets the fork at `engine.current_tick()`, replicate 0 returns without setting anything.
- Part 1: `Lineage { key, births }` (`sim_core::components::lineage`) is attached only when `keyed_draws` is set: founders `Lineage::founder(seed, spawn_index)`, newborns `first_parent.child(seed)` (the first of the two parents the birth code already picks, the lowest `AgentId` member; its `births` advances). If the first parent carries no lineage (not reachable in a keyed world) the key falls back to `keyed_u64(seed, STREAM_NEWBORN, settlement_id, tick, u32::MAX, 0)`. Attaching the component is the only place Part 1 reads the flag. Check: `game_v15_scene` with only the lineage attached gives the same `state_digest` as `game_v14_scene` at every day for 28 days on seeds 0–3 (an ad-hoc release check, not kept as a test because Part 2 makes v15 differ).
- Part 1, known limitation of lineage keys (6.3, not hidden): in a one-settlement village the first parent is nearly always the same founder, so `births` is in practice the village's birth order; an added or removed birth shifts the keys of every later newborn of that parent. The keys still never read the global `AgentId` counter and become parent-specific once first parents differ (two settlements, a dead founder). Y6 reports how often newborn keys match across fork arms; it is not gated.
- Part 1: probe schema 9 = schema 8 rows with `schema_version` 9 (no row key added) and two keys in the summary's `rules` block: `keyed_draws` (0/1) and `noise_replicate` (the replicate in force at the fresh engine's tick, 0 for every probe run, which never forks). Recorder schema 3: with `--agents`, `agent_keys.npy` (uint64 [N], aligned with `agent_ids.npy`, 0 without a lineage) with field `lineage_key` marked `hidden` and `"identity": true` (so it is not in `observable_fields`); `meta.json` gains `keyed_draws` and `noise_fork`; `episode_matches` (resume) also compares `noise_fork`. `--fork-tick T --replicates R` (needs `--schedule-dir` and `--include-factual`; R in 1..=64) expands every episode into R members `<scene>_s<seed>_<id>_r<k>`; each member runs from tick 0 and applies `apply_noise_fork(k)` when the loop reaches tick T, after row T is taken and before that tick's interventions, so row T is pre-fork. Members are independent runs (no engine clone), so each costs a full episode.
- Part 1: `wm_data.py` accepts schema 3 (`SCHEMAS = (1, 2, 3)`, `ep.lineage_keys()`). Its tests load the committed schema-1 sample, a freshly recorded `game_v15` schema-3 episode, the same episode rewritten to schema 2's layout, and (when present) a real dataset-v1 schema-2 episode. `docs/worldmodel/README.md` documents schema 3.
- Part 1: `evaluate_pass9.py` calls `evaluate_pass8.evaluate` unchanged on schema-9 rounds (same clauses, thresholds and comparison mode); its X8 timing baseline is a same-host `game_v14` summary. `fork_metrics.py` implements section 8 (placebo identity, VRF with the cluster bootstrap, sign stability, spread, the v14 comparison, divergence horizon, newborn key matches) and imports `bench_v1.c_cf_targets` unchanged. Identity (8.1) also ignores `meta.schedule`, after checking that every scheduled row has amount 0 (a placebo's schedule necessarily differs from the factual's null). Infinite VRFs are written to `fork_metrics.json` as `Infinity` (Python's json reads them back).
- Part 1 ended 11:15 KST; Part 2 started 11:15.
- Part 2 (7.1): idle Brownian movement under `keyed_draws` draws pause = `keyed_unit_f64(seed, STREAM_MOVE_PAUSE, key, tick, 0, r)` (only when `idle_pause_chance > 0`, as v14), dx / dy = `keyed_step` on `STREAM_MOVE_DX` / `STREAM_MOVE_DY` with the same key, `r = noise_replicate_for(tick)`. A paused agent skips dx/dy, which shifts nothing (each draw is its own key). The `MovementRng` component stays attached and is never advanced in v15 (tested). A mover without a `Lineage` (none in a v15 game world; only hand-built test worlds) keeps the sequential stream; recorded rather than keyed on subject 0, which would make every key-less mover step identically. v14's `% 3` and v15's multiply-shift `below(·, 3)` have the same distribution (bias < 2⁻⁶²) but give different values.
- Part 2 (7.1): lifespans under `keyed_draws` come from `keyed_u64(seed, STREAM_LIFESPAN, lineage key, 0, slot, r_b)` with multiply-shift instead of `%`: founders slot 0 = age, 1–3 = lifespan, 4 = top-up (always drawn, used only when needed); newborns slots 1–3 (`keyed_newborn_life`). `r_b` = `noise_replicate_for(birth tick)`; founders use tick 0. With the game's aging values the founder top-up can never fire (oldest founder 57,600 + minimum remainder 4,320 < shortest lifespan 72,000), so the data-dependent count of v14 was latent; the fixed slot is tested with older founders.
- Part 2 (7.1, order): under `keyed_draws` the decision system's main loop and its SeekTarget post-pass collect their rows and process them in `AgentId` order (O1, O2); starvation/dehydration deaths are booked in `AgentId` order (O3); construction completions are sorted by position also without shelter planning (O4, already sorted in every game ruleset); settlement history routing breaks exact distance ties by settlement id (O6) and routes new buildings in `BuildingId` order (O7). Memory insertion order (O5) needs no change once its emitters are canonical. v8–v14 keep hecs and HashMap order (every change is flag-gated). Collecting the rows into a `Vec` does not change hecs order, so v14 is unaffected; the Part 2 guard confirms it.

A first evaluation reported 192 replicate-0 failures: a defect in my identity check, which required every scheduled row to have amount 0 (right for placebos, wrong for a real variant compared with its own unforked run). Fixed: schedules are compared after removing amount-0 rows, symmetric with the `interventions.json` rule; a test covers both cases. No episode was re-recorded.
- Part 3 G0b (8.2): **FAIL**, recorded and not tuned. Dark nights: VRF = 1.00 for gift_wood, rain, cold_snap and windfall and 1.19 [1.00, 1.70] for rot_wood. The reason is structural: in 29 of 32 seeds the factual's dark-night count is the same in all 8 replicates (agent noise does not reach it), and where the factual is constant within a seed the paired and unpaired differences have identical variance, so that seed contributes VRF 1; the remaining variance is noise that exists only in the variant arm (effect heterogeneity across noise realisations), which no pairing removes. It is not the "no agent noise" exclusion of 8.2 (V_p = V_u = 0) because 1–7 seeds are noisy in the variant arm. Cold member-hours: VRF 1.54–2.17; lower bound > 1 for rot_wood (1.16), rain (1.09), cold_snap (1.14), windfall (1.40), not for gift_wood (0.94). Reported only: food store VRF 3.6–24.9 (all lower bounds > 2), deaths and births at day 29 06:00 never differ across replicates or arms (no agent noise). The report adds, per variant and target, the number of seeds noisy in each arm and a reported-only VRF over the seeds noisy in both arms (gift_wood cold hours 2.78, dark nights 1.0–3.3 on 0–1 seeds).
- Part 3 (8.3): the two v14 re-records are byte-identical to dataset v1 under the Pass 8 recorder (every file) and `.npy`-identical under the Pass 9 recorder (meta differs only by `schema_version` and the new keys). The v14 single-fork comparison is in `fork_metrics.md`: mean |Δ_v14 − mean_k Δ_v15| is 0.25–0.43 dark nights and 58–100 cold member-hours, against v15 within-seed spreads (median std) of 0 and 0–15: the v14 → v15 difference of a single fork is mostly trajectory difference from tick 0 (v15's prefix differs), not noise reassignment.
- Part 3 (8.4): divergence at replicate 0 (medians over seeds): the first agent position differs 60 ticks (windfall) to 2,550 ticks (cold_snap) after the intervention and 4,440 ticks after the one-unit gift (3 seeds never); the moved share depends on the hour (at night everyone is in bed, so positions coincide). Targets: rain changes the fire within 60 ticks; gift_wood / rot_wood / windfall / cold_snap change dark nights and cold hours after 2,040–7,830 ticks (gift_wood never in 8 seeds, windfall never in 8); the one-unit gift never changes cold hours and changes dark nights in 3 seeds; deaths never differ in 35 days. Agent-level noise changes where villagers stand within an hour but rarely changes village outcomes in the 35-day window.

- Part 5 (10, 12:22): `make_dataset.py families --name NAME --scene S --seeds a..b --days N --fork-tick T --replicates R --schedule-dir DIR --family-splits train=a..b,... [--agents]` records one directory per root seed (`ROOT/NAME/<scene>_s<seed>/`, one recorder call per seed via `export.sh --resume`) holding the members `<scene>_s<seed>_<id>_r<k>`, and writes `ROOT/NAME/families_manifest.json` (per family: root seed, split, scene, fork tick, R, members with schedule id, replicate, path and file sha256s, checked against each `meta.json`). Splits are given per root seed and validated (every seed in exactly one split), so a family's members never cross splits. `wm_data.load_family(path)` returns a `Family` (seed, scene, fork tick, R, `factual[k]`, `variants[id][k]`) and rejects members that disagree on scene, seed or fork tick, whose directory suffix differs from `noise_fork.replicate`, or that miss a replicate. Tests (`tools/worldmodel/test_families.py`) record a tiny family set (2 seeds × 2 variants + factual × 2 replicates × 3 days) through the real command and check the manifest, splits, replicate loading, the shared prefix up to the fork, divergence after it, and that founders' lineage keys agree across members. No v2 dataset is recorded.

A first reviewer finished but its final message only pointed to an earlier message that never reached this session, so its findings were unavailable; a second reviewer with the same brief wrote its report to a file (`docs/revitalize/probe/p9-6_review/review_round1.md`) — that is round 1. It re-implemented `keyed_u64` in Python and checked that all 1,845 newborn keys in 61 sampled fork episodes lie in the founders' child closure (no fallback key), and recomputed V_p, V_u and VRF from the recorded episodes (equal to `fork_metrics.json`). No High finding.
  1. M1 (Medium) — `harness_v15_newborns_carry_their_first_parents_key` did not check that keys come from the first parent (a silently failing parent lookup would give every newborn the settlement/tick fallback key and the test would still pass). **Fixed**: the test now requires every newborn key of a 28-day v15 run to lie in the child closure of the founders' keys (three generations).
  2. L1 — the divergence table's "after 1 h / 6 h / 24 h / 72 h" read rows 0/5/23/71 (row 0 is the intervention tick, pre-intervention). **Fixed**: the row is looked up by its tick (`start + 60 × hours`); the table is regenerated (e.g. windfall after 1 h 0.405, previously shown as 0).
  3. L2 — AgentId order remains a residual cross-arm dependence: decision and death order, the first parent and the "lowest-AgentId peer" picks follow AgentIds, which come from the global counter, so an extra birth in one arm renumbers that arm's later newborns. **Documented** (here and in the report): no key contains an AgentId, and the relative order of agents matched across arms changes only if their birth order differs.
  4. L3 — `--fork-tick 0` was accepted although founders' lives are drawn at worldgen before the fork is set. **Fixed**: the recorder rejects tick 0 (`1..days × 1440`), with a test.
  5. L4 — the exact-zero variance test is safe only for integer targets and power-of-two R. **Fixed**: a variance ≤ 1e-12 × max(1, mean square) counts as 0; test with a constant fractional column.
  6. L5 — a bootstrap resample with no agent noise at all was counted as +∞. **Fixed**: such resamples are excluded and counted (`bootstrap_quiet_resamples`); test added. Effect on the committed numbers: only the upper bound of rain's dark-night VRF changes (∞ → 1.0); no gate result changes.
  7. L6 — a missing unforked run was skipped silently in the replicate-0 identity. **Fixed**: a missing run counts as a failure (the committed run has all 224).
  8. L7 — two completed construction sites on one tile would keep HashMap order in the position sort (the inventory's O8, never produced by shelter planning). **Documented**.
- Part 6 review, round 2 (fresh `general-purpose` subagent, read-only, on `ba278357`; report `docs/revitalize/probe/p9-6_review/review_round2.md`): M1, L1, L3, L4, L5, L6 confirmed fixed; L2 documented accurately; no High finding. Its findings, all handled (review rounds used: 2 of 2):
  1. R2-1 (Medium, process) — no gate had run on the fixed code. **Done**: the full gate runs on the final head (`docs/revitalize/verification/p9-6/gate.txt`).
  2. R2-2 — the M1 closure built four levels (15.75 M keys) while its comment said three. **Fixed**: three levels (founders, 400 children each, 40 per child; ≈ 394 k keys), which still covers a 28-day village (≈ 22–26 births, nearly all from one founder).
  3. R2-3 — the L4 floor scaled with the values' level (it could hide a ±1 difference at level 1e5) and the noisy-seed counts used an exact test. **Fixed**: one per-seed rule for both — a seed's replicates are constant when their range is ≤ 1e-9 × max(1, largest |value|); tests for level 1e5 and fractional rows.
  4. R2-4 — the excluded all-quiet resamples were not visible. **Fixed**: docstring and table text say so, and the table shows the count next to each interval (rain dark nights: 96 of 2,000; round 1's ≤ 1.3e-3 estimate was low, (29/32)^32 ≈ 4.3 %). Dropping those resamples can only lower a lower bound, so G0b can only get stricter.
  5. R2-5 — G0a could pass with nothing compared. **Fixed**: G0a requires at least one placebo and a replicate-0 comparison for every family (`replicate0_expected`).
  6. R2-6 — L1 and L6 had no unit test. **Fixed**: `test_moved_share_rows_are_found_by_tick`, `test_missing_unforked_run_fails`.
  7. R2-7 — L7 overstated: `completed` holds at most one entity per position (progress comes from the position-keyed `sites_by_pos`), so the sort at `construction_system.rs:201` cannot tie; what remains latent is only O8 itself (which entity wins at `:111-113` when two sites share a tile), a `std` HashMap order that would break run-to-run determinism as well. **Reworded** here.
- Part 6 final reproduction (12:40–13:10, `probe_p2`; no simulation Rust changed after `874dbf54`): legacy, v8, v9–v13 (10 and 120 days) and v14 (10 and 120 days) with Pass 8's flags (`final_probes.sh`) reproduce Pass 8's final summaries on every shared value (4,847 to 57,656 per run); the only differences are `schema_version` 8 → 9 and the two new rules keys; all 14 determinism twins pass. The final v15 runs are the Part 2 tuning and Part 4 held-out runs (same Rust).

- Part 1 (16:11–): `ExposureRule { damage_per_cold_hour: [f64; 3], heal_block_hours: u32 }` lives in `sim_core::rules` and `SocietyRules::exposure` (None in default and v8–v15, so every frozen literal gains `exposure: None`); `WorldRules::game_v16_neutral()` = `game_v15()` + the start values [9, 6, 9] / 2 h. `ExposureClock { last_cold_tick }` (`sim_core::components::exposure_clock`) with `blocks_healing(tick, hours)` = `tick < last_cold_tick + 60 + 60 × hours` (the marked hour plus the block, as 6.2 states).
- Part 1: the damage is applied inside `FireSystem` right after the markers are set (`fire.rs` `expose`): every agent marked `Cold` at this run — newly or already — in AgentId order; stage = `Child` → 0, else `Elder` → 2, else 1; the clock is updated in place when present (inserted otherwise, so an archetype move happens at most once per agent); `apply_damage` then `is_dead`; deaths collected and booked through `despawn_agent(…, DeathReason::Exposure, now)` in AgentId order after the loop (never despawning mid-iteration). Without the rule `expose` is never called, so v8–v15 are unchanged (the inert test and the 4.6 guard confirm it).
- Part 1: `StarvationSystem`'s heal branch gains one condition under the rule (`!(Cold || clock.blocks_healing)`); the query adds `Option<&Cold>, Option<&ExposureClock>`, which does not change hecs iteration order. Starvation and dehydration damage are unchanged.
- Part 1: `DeathReason::Exposure` (`as_str` "exposure", `as_u8` 4) is counted in `despawn_agent` (`Tallies::deaths_exposure`), the FFI tallies dictionary (`deaths_exposure`), the probe census (`probe/deaths.rs`: the recorded reason is read before the saturation inference, `cross_check` and `tallies()` have 5 entries), the probe rows (`deaths_exposure`), the Demography table (an Exposure column), the recorder's `global.npy` (`deaths_exposure`), and the three exhaustive test matches. `fork_metrics.py` gains its flag in Part 6 (section 12).
- Part 1: measurement-only additions to `SimResources`: `world_variety: Option<WorldVariety>` (None outside `game_v16`, the `WorldVariety` type is defined here so the instruments can report it; the draw follows in Part 2) and `worldgen_report: WorldGenReport` (deadwood spots requested / placed, berry-repair swaps; set by worldgen, never read by a system and not part of any digest).
- Part 1, probe schema 10: three row keys (`deaths_exposure`, `min_hp` over living agents, `injured_members` = settlement members with hp < max); per seed in `summary.json` `runs[]`: `deaths_exposure`, `seed_info` (world variety or null, the seed's own severity weights and food regrowth permille, starting agents, deadwood requested / placed, berry-repair swaps, the exposure rule), `exposure_death_ticks` (system ticks = census tick − 1), `seed_winters` (D = 22, 50, 78, 106 whose window [D 18:00, D+7 07:00) fits the run: members and alive at D 18:00, dark nights = evenings D..D+6 with the fire out at the next 02:00 sample, severity at D+1 02:00, exposure deaths whose system tick is in the window), `dawn_health` ([day, min_hp, injured, alive] at every 06:00 sample), `alive_daily` and `settlements_daily` (08:00 checkpoints). The `rules` block still comes from the first seed only; nothing per-seed is read from it.
- Part 1: `probe/runner.rs`'s two determinism checks now call one function `twin_checked(scene)` (instead of two copies of the `matches!` list) that names both v16 scenes; a test asserts both call sites use it and that both v16 scenes are accepted, so a scene cannot silently skip one check.
- Part 1, recorder schema 4: `global.npy` appends `deaths_exposure`, `injured_members` (columns 35, 36), `agents.npy` appends `hp` (field 17, observable), `meta.json` gains `world_variety` (null, or `{"hidden": true, "values": {…}}`); `wm_data.py` reads schemas 1–4, `Episode.observable_meta()` drops a top-level meta block marked hidden, `Episode.world_variety()` returns the values; `load_family` accepts schema-3 and schema-4 members. Columns are appended (never inserted) so every schema-1–3 column index is unchanged; `bench_v0.py` / `bench_v1.py` are untouched.
- Part 1, `evaluate_pass10.py`: H1–H6 and H10 from schema-10 runs; H5 calls `evaluate_pass8.evaluate` unchanged on the v16 run and on the v15 run of the same seeds. - Part 1 choices: `rain_soak_permille` lives in `FirewoodRule` (the goal's first option); `InterventionRecord::soaked_wood` (no serde derives; the recorder writes it into every `interventions.json` row, 0 outside v17). `Hardiness` is attached when `ExposureRule::use_hardiness` is set and the resident has a `Lineage` (every v17 resident: founders at worldgen, newborns at birth); its value `700 + keyed_below(seed, STREAM_HARDINESS, lineage key, 0, 0, 0, 601)`, replicate 0 always. Exposure v2 is one function `fire::exposure_damage` (both factors skipped at v16's values, so v16 is bit-identical). The death census adds `stage` (child / adult / elder, read before despawn) and `settlement_at_death`; `life_stage_at_death` is unchanged.
- Part 1/2 versioning: `WorldVariety` gains slots 8–10 (`wild_regions` 1–3, `region_bushes` 5–9, `region_deadwood` 6–12; `NEUTRAL` 0). v16 never reads them and v16 scenes record `draw(seed).without_regions()`, so every v16 output (probe `seed_info`, recorder meta) is unchanged; the probe and recorder print the region fields only when `wild_regions > 0`. New stream constants `STREAM_HARDINESS`, `STREAM_REGION`, `STREAM_REGION_BUSH`, `STREAM_REGION_WOOD`, `STREAM_FISSION` (in `STREAMS`, distinctness tested).
- Part 2 wild regions (`camp::regions`, after camp, berries and deadwood, so a v17 world's camp is v16's): centre uniform by `keyed_below(seed, STREAM_REGION, 0, 0, r, 0, n)` over walkable non-water tiles 22–28 from the camp hearth and ≥ 20 from earlier centres, (y, x) order; bushes = the camp berries' candidate filter (Forest with a Plain neighbour, no building/water/bush) within 2–6 of the centre, shuffled by `STREAM_REGION_BUSH` (slot r, swap index as the tick field); deadwood = the camp deadwood filter (Forest then Plain, no water/building/food, ≥ 2 from every spot) shuffled by `STREAM_REGION_WOOD` (Plain list on slot r + 65,536). Shortfalls and the centre's distance to water are counted (`WorldGenReport`, `SimResources::wild_regions`), never asserted; terrain is never edited (the berry repair is not run for regions).
- Part 2 regional wood (`FirewoodRule::regional_wood`, v17 only): the winter fall's world total is split per area (camp area 0, region r → r + 1, `SimResources::wood_spot_area`) in proportion to its spot count, each area with its own accumulator and round-robin cursor (`wood_area_acc`, `wood_area_cursor`); windfall sorts spots by Chebyshev distance to the home settlement's formation tile (then key). v8–v16 keep the world-total round robin and key-order windfall.
- Part 2 fission (`FissionSystem`, priority 148 after `FireSystem`, interval 1): the real work is gated to whole hours (remnants) and 06:00 (the daily check); founding progress is checked every tick so the explicit membership move happens on the very tick the formation scan forms the daughter (goal 7.2 step 5). Recorded deviations and choices: (1) a founding is stamped like `camp.rs::populate` (Hearth + two Shelters, nearest buildable tile, `building_event_queue`, sleep tiles), the formation scan then forms the daughter and assigns it the shelters (Owned housing); (2) a placed founding with no daughter one day later, or a group with fewer than three living members (the formation threshold), is cancelled (markers removed, store returned, hearth retired); (3) departed groups count toward `max_settlements` and a parent with a group on its way does not split again; (4) founders' state: Idle or Seeking members are set Idle with their `SeekTarget` dropped at departure so the walk starts at once, Consuming members finish first; (5) `SettlementSystem`'s formation scan ignores the anchors of settlements that no longer exist (`FissionSystem` dissolves settlements itself; before Pass 11 both maps always hold the same ids, so v8–v16 are unchanged); (6) explicit dissolution uses the existing `DissolutionCause::EmptyMembersAndBuildings` (no new variant allowed) with `parent: None`.
- Part 2 sticky membership (audit g8; a decision against the goal's assumption that the existing re-assignment stays): under `FissionRule` the proximity sync admits only agents that belong to no settlement. Evidence, scratch seeds 900000–900011 (start values, 120 days): with the existing rule `resolve_exclusivity` moved members between villages 0–2,667 times per seed (median ≈ 600: foragers and wood gatherers of one village walking within 5 tiles of the other's hearth flip back and forth, so each village's roster stopped meaning anything); with sticky membership 0–5 per seed. `Tallies::exclusivity_moves` counts the moves outside fission in every version (FissionSystem subtracts the founders the formation scan moved) and the probe reports it per run (J-report).
- Part 2 walk: founders hold `Seeking { Agent(leader) }`, the post-pass gives every founder (leader included) `SeekTarget = site`; the social gate, chase timeout and handshake skip `Founding` agents; an actionable need (the decision helper's) pre-empts the walk (marker kept, site target dropped) and the next Idle decision re-enters the walk before every other arm. `Founding` agents are excluded from builder assignment, the forage/firewood/meal arms (they never reach them), meals served by `StockpileConsumeSystem`, the tethers and haul-home of `movement.rs`, and the parent's `Cold` set. Beds are kept: a group still walking at night sleeps in its parent's shelters (rare: groups leave at 06:00 and the walk is about 25 tiles).
- Part 2 instruments: probe schema 11 (8 row keys; per seed `pass11` block: fission log, settlements with founding tick, parent, founders, members at days 30/60/90/118, dissolution tick, alive 28 days after founding, exposure deaths by stage per seed-winter with elders at the winter's start, the most exposure deaths in one hour per settlement-night, the longest 1–2-member stretch; `settlements_alive_daily`; per-seed `seed_info` gains `wild_regions`, `exposure_v2`, `firewood_v17`, `fission`, `sustain_utilization` only in v17 worlds). Recorder schema 5: global `settlements_alive`, `fissions_total`; settlements `parent_slot`, `founded_tick`; agents `hardiness` (hidden) and `founding`; the hidden meta block adds the region centres; `wm_data.py` accepts schema 5. `evaluate_pass11.py` (J1–J6, J9, J12), `diversity_p11.py` (dyn-η² etc.), `fork_metrics.py --home-slot-fire`.

Helper scripts `run_probe.sh`, `run_set.sh`, `cmp.sh`, `guard.sh` (4.6: game_v17, game_v17_nofission, game_v16 tuning sets) are Pass 11's with the paths changed.
- Part 0 frozen pins: `harness_revitalize_pass12_frozen.rs` pins v17 and v17_nofission at 1,440 and 40,320 ticks (fingerprint, digest, births, fissions founded); at 40,320 the two scenes differ for seeds 1 and 2 (their first group left at 06:00 of display day 29 and is still walking), so the pins see the fission path. The file was committed in the form that compiles against the base (Part 2's struct-field additions came later, as allowed literal additions).
- Part 0 UBS (5.2): the LOO is an explicit leave-one-out refit (closed form over the fold's sums, verified against an explicit refit in a unit test); features are standardized over the episodes in use; the intercept is unpenalized. The paired bootstrap refits at the λ chosen on the full set and leaves out every copy of a duplicated seed together (a duplicated seed never predicts itself). The nonlinear synthetic map has to appear after day 10: a static nonlinear effect present from day 3 is carried by the early-level feature m_e and gives UBS ≈ 0 (first version of the test; recorded so the limitation is stated precisely: the ridge misses nonlinear static effects that are not already in the early level).
- Part 0 daughter diagnosis: sources are Pass 11's final probe runs (`p11-7_final_v17_120day`, `p11-7_final_heldout_v17_120day`), which reproduce the frozen R3 and Part 4 runs on every shared key; all 96 seeds (not only those with deaths) were recorded with `p11bin/wm_final` into `worldsim-wm-data/p12/diag/game_v17/` (no `--agents`). Per-settlement-winter ratios use the recorder's members at 18:00 of the winter's first day (the J5 definition of 7.6/10). Results and the fixes they select: `DAUGHTER_DIAGNOSIS.md`.
- Part 0 ended 11:56 KST (commits `6de4adaf` goal, `4bf98c14` pins/UBS/diagnosis; the baseline reproduction table and these notes in the Part 0 docs commit).
- Part 1 (11:52–12:57 KST): the ablation is a probe-only scene list (`abl_*`, `fwd_*` in `sim-test/src/probe/ablation.rs`); the activity breakdown is a schema-12 probe row key set (`adult_act_*`, every row; the 15:00 clock rows are what Part 1 reads). Two sub-bisection scenes (regions with only bushes / only deadwood) and a hypothesis check (`abl_local_food`, v17_nofission with the Part 2 flag) were added after the first results; the check scene was run three times as the flag grew (availability only → + meal target → + Gather arm), each run with its own frozen binary (`probe_p1b`, `p1c`, `p1d`) and label, all disclosed in BISECTION.md. Because `abl_local_food` needs the Part 2 field, the final `ablation.rs` is committed with Part 2.
- Part 1 finding (one difference explains it): the region bushes; Part 2 fixes it for v18 behind `AgentRules::local_food_availability` (7.5). Its first two versions did not restore the gap (0.03, −0.05); the third (Gather arm in the catchment) restores 0.71. These were mechanism iterations in Part 2's scope (no knob, no judged round).
- Part 2 hazard (7.1): `FissionRule` gains `allowed_seasons` and `hazard: Option<FissionHazard>`; `FissionHazard::is_valid` (size_hi > size_lo, crowd_hi > crowd_lo, weights sum 1000, ceiling ≤ 1000) is `debug_assert!`ed in `game_v18_with` and unit-tested. `fission::hazard_terms` is the formula in i64 thousandths (only `adult_need_per_day` is f64, floored once inside `need`); the draw is `keyed_below(seed, STREAM_FISSION_HAZARD, sid, tick, 0, r, 1000)` with r the replicate in force; split iff draw < h. The check order is Pass 11's: the hazard replaces the crowding gate after the capacity, before the site choice; a successful draw with no free site counts `fission_blocked_no_site`. The two early `return`s of Pass 11's loop (world cooldown, `max_settlements`) became `break` so the checks logged before them are kept (nothing follows the loop, so this is behaviour-identical; the v17 pins confirm). Every check that reaches the draw is logged in `SimResources::fission_checks` (measurement only, not in the digest). v18 start values as 7.1 says.
- Part 2 region fill (7.2): implemented as 7.2 says, plus a **tier 5** (any Plain tile in the ring 2–9, beyond the camp's reach; `STREAM_REGION_FILL_BUSH` slot r + (5 << 16)). Reason: with tiers 2–4 v18 seeds 0–127 realized 2,369 of 2,519 bushes (0.940 < J9's 0.95); the short regions hold no Forest within reach (ring 2–9 is Plain/Hill, e.g. seed 54 region (6, 51): 295 Plain tiles, 0 Forest), so no tier that needs Forest can fill them. With tier 5: 2,519 / 2,519 bushes, deadwood 3,692 / 3,716. Terrain, water and buildings are never touched (test). Tier counts in `WorldGenReport::region_fill_bushes` ([u32; 4], tiers 2–5) and `region_fill_deadwood`.
- Part 2 daughter fixes (7.3), from DAUGHTER_DIAGNOSIS.md's counts: (a) `region_water_max` Some(12); (b) `RANGES_V18` region bushes 8–12, region deadwood 12–18; (c) `area_fall_floor_permille` 600 with `SETTLED_REGION_RADIUS` 9; (d) not implemented (no daughter was founded off a region centre). The floor adds `max(per_day × 1000 × n_area, per_day × floor × n_camp)` per pulse to a settled area's accumulator; its extra is counted (`Tallies::area_floor_wood`, units added before the snow cap) and units lost to the cap in settled regions (`winter_wood_lost_settled`). Finding while testing: with region deadwood 12–18 the floor at 600 binds only where a region has fewer than 0.6 × the camp's spots (seed 11: region 14 vs camp 26 spots, six winter days with spots emptied after every pulse: 29 units with the floor vs 26 without; at 1000: 49 vs 26) — a knob for Part 3.
- Part 2 wet burn (7.4): `FirewoodRule::rain_wet_burn`; under it a raining settlement inside the burn window withdraws `per_hour` (all it holds, if less) into `Tallies::wet_burn_wood` and stays unlit; the soak at application is unchanged. Probe row key `wet_burn_wood_total`, FFI tallies key `wet_burn_wood`.
- Part 2 J6 fix (7.5), from Part 1 (the regions explain the whole gap; mechanism below): `AgentRules::local_food_availability` (v8–v17 false, v18 true): a settlement member's food availability is whether a food tile with food lies within its settlement's catchment radius (`food_economy::catchment_radius`, 16) of the formation tile; non-members keep the world-wide check.
- Part 2 instruments: probe schema 12 (row keys `adult_act_*`, `wet_burn_wood_total`, `area_floor_wood_total`; per seed `pass12`: every logged hazard check, first draw day per settlement, settlement-winters with members at 18:00 of display days 22/50/78/106 and exposure deaths in the window, fill counts, the Pass 12 tallies). Recorder schema stays 5 (no new array column needed; `game_v18`, `game_v18_nofission` added to `SCENES`/dispatch/`USAGE`). `evaluate_pass12.py` (J3 with the display-day conversion, the 0.25 peak clause, h terciles and parameter correlations; J4 0.70 with founding-season shares; J5 per settlement-winter; J9 on `RANGES_V18` and realized regions), `test_evaluate_pass12.py`.
- Part 2: the live game builds `game_v18` (both `world_node.rs` constructors); the FFI tallies dictionary gains `wet_burn_wood`. No function named `get_intervention_state` / `get_society_snapshot` added; no field the art branch's getter reads was renamed or removed.
- Part 2 J6 fix, final form (after Part 1's three versions): under `local_food_availability` a member's food availability, its meal target (`nearest_local_food`) and its Gather-arm tile (`nearest_gather_tile`) count only food tiles within `catchment_radius` (16) of its settlement's formation tile; maps built once per decision tick in settlement-id order, empty without the flag. Finding: 193 of 195 dead children were born inside the winter window (the child stage lasts 4 days and births barely slow in winter); one fully dark 13-hour night costs a child 104 hp (8 per cold hour) and an adult 65. Two of the four over-0.35 settlement-winters are young daughters with newborns (39, 77), two are camps that entered the winter with stock 0 and 57 after an autumn split took their adults (66, 24). Removing the newborn deaths arithmetically moves the adult share to 0.83 (> J5's 0.50 bound). Decision (7.3): **no J5 mechanism** — the evidence points at two different mechanisms (newborns, adult labour in autumn) and a newborn gate would break J5's adult-share clause; the child-share clause is left to the exposure-damage knob of Part 3 (child 5–10).

The key is `keyed_below(seed, STREAM_FISSION_THRESHOLD, sid, 0, k, 0, 10⁶)` (new stream "FISSTHRS"); the replicate slot is the constant 0. A split blocked by no site or by group formation keeps P and θ (the code `continue`s after the update). After a recorded split P = 0 (`reset_on_split`) and θ is redrawn with k = the parent's `fission_log` records (the new one included). θ of a new settlement is drawn in `run_formation_scan` (k = the id's records, 0 for a fresh id). A settlement whose θ is 0 at a pressure check (only possible for a settlement deserialized from before Pass 13) draws it there with the same key — never reached in any run of this pass, kept for saved games of the live build.

The natural mode builds its world with the probe's own scene lookup: `probe::runner::scene_engine` is widened from `pub(super)` to `pub(crate)` and re-exported `pub(crate)` from `probe/mod.rs` (a visibility change only).

- Part 1 (instruments), 22:50 onward. **6.1** `TickProfile` holds a `Vec<SystemProfile>` (new type: `name`, `priority`, `interval`, `calls`, `total_ns`, `max_ns`) in execution order plus `ticks`, `tick_us`, `prune_ns`, `last_tick_ns`. `tick()` is `tick_inner(None)`; `tick_inner` keeps today's statements in today's order and reads `Instant` only through `profile.as_ref().map(..)` / the `Some(p)` arm. The whole-tick time runs from before `resources.current_tick` is set to after the clock update; it is rounded to the nearest µs (`(ns + 500) / 1000`, saturating at `u32::MAX`) rather than truncated, so short ticks (≈ 100 µs) carry no −0.5 µs bias. A profile whose system count differs is replaced by `new_tick_profile()` before timing (documented, no panic). `SimEngine` gains no field.
- **6.2** `WorldGenConfig::stress_v19(seed, agents, side)` is a new `const fn` (no new field). The stress world does not set `SimResources::world_variety` (that field is measurement only — read by the recorder's metadata and tests, never by a system); the game's own `WorldSimNode::init` / `reset_world` do not set it either.
- **6.3** `worldsim_perf` (`sim-test/src/bin/worldsim_perf.rs`, logic in `sim-test/src/perf.rs`; no new dependency, `Cargo.lock` unchanged). Conventions fixed here: (a) the per-system figures (`calls`, `total_ms`, `mean_us_per_call`, `max_us`, `share_of_tick_time`), the tick summary and the slowest ticks are over the steady ticks (tick ≥ 60); the totals including the warm-up are also written (`*_incl_warmup`), and the warm-up's own summary and tick 0 are reported apart; (b) `share_of_tick_time` = steady system time / steady whole-tick time; the rest is `prune_ms` and `unaccounted_ms` (loop, `Instant` reads); (c) percentiles are nearest-rank (`ceil(p/100 × n)`, computed in integer arithmetic so p99.9 of 1,000 values is the 999th), the same in `perf.rs` and `perf_p15.py`; (d) a natural run is named with its founders count and feed `none` (`natural_game_v19_s15000_n<founders>_side64_none`); the sidecar is `<stem>.tick_us.bin` (every tick including the warm-up); (e) the fed gift's recorded `tick` is `InterventionRecord::tick` (completed ticks when applied, i.e. the next tick's number); the 06:00 sample and gift are taken after a tick whose end clock reads 06:00, the sample before the gift; (f) per-day rows carry their tick count, so the analysis can keep only full days (day 0 starts at 08:00 and is partial); (g) worldgen and the run itself run under `catch_unwind`; a failure is a `worldgen_failed` / `run_failed` row in `runs.json`, never a missing number; (h) the 20-minute size limit (7.2) is enforced by the binary: after a run longer than 1,200 s the rest of that size and every larger size are skipped and a `size_limit` row is written; (i) `runs.json` is merged by stem, so the fed and raw sweeps share one directory.
- **6.4** `build_stress_engine(seed, agents)` (pure) and `#[func] reset_world_stress(seed, agents)` in `world_node.rs`; nothing in `scripts/` calls it (grep).
- **6.5** The caller inventory was made by a fresh `general-purpose` subagent (read-only, both branches: working tree `scripts/` and `git grep` on `origin/codex/art-production-2026-10` = `56059e0f`) and spot-checked (WIP `hud_topbar.gd` `_process → _refresh` L97–103; art `building_renderer.gd` `_animate_hearths` L311–320 calls `get_time_snapshot()` twice per hearth). Per-frame on this branch: 19 + 2T getter calls per frame (6 × `get_agent_snapshot`, 3 × `get_settlement_snapshot`, 2 × `get_tile_causal_history` per distinct agent tile, …); on the art branch 62 + 7H + 2T, including two getters that do not exist here (`get_society_snapshot` 5 per frame, `get_intervention_state` 4 per frame), which therefore cannot be timed in this pass and are listed as such. `snapshot_timings` times every getter's Rust body (not only the per-frame ones), 100 calls each, at the end of every natural and stress run: `get_agent_snapshot` = `collect_agent_snapshot` + `collect_agent_ages_days` + `collect_agent_colds`; `get_settlement_snapshot` = `collect_settlement_snapshot` + `collect_settlement_wood_targets`; `get_influence_overlay` is timed as a copy of one channel's buffer into a `Vec` (a stand-in for the `PackedByteArray` copy); `get_tile_causal_history` is timed once at the first agent's tile and once as a whole pass over every distinct agent tile (the renderer's per-frame loop). `context` records agents, distinct agent tiles (T), buildings, hearths (H), settlements and construction sites for the ×callers figure. The macOS clock behind `Instant` ticks in ≈ 41.7 ns steps, so the cheapest getters read 0.000 / 0.042 / 0.083 µs (at the clock's resolution).
- Timed batches go through `tools/revitalize/perf_batch_p15.sh <log> <command>`: it records date, load averages and `pgrep -fl` before the batch, and starts only when the 1-minute load is < 4 and no `Godot`, `cargo`, `rustc`, `clippy-driver` or other `worldsim_*` process runs (by name); otherwise it checks every 10 minutes and after 2 hours runs anyway with `PERF_CONTENDED=1`, which every run JSON records as `contended`.

Unit tests over 20,000 keys: founder mean within 500 ± 5 and SD within 167 ± 5 per axis; child slope on mid-parent 0.45–0.55 and child SD 167 ± 10 for independent founder parents.
