- **Insertion (6.4 a)**: inside the existing keyed-draw (lineage) blocks of `worldgen/camp.rs` and `settlement_system.rs`, right after the `Hardiness` insert, only when `rules.agent.temperament.is_some()`; the RD rate is a mutation of the `Social` already inserted (`get::<&mut Social>`), so no archetype changes beyond the one new component. Newborn parents: the `Temperament` of the two `parent_ids` the birth already names (looked up with one `(&Agent, &Temperament)` query per birth, before the spawn; a parent without the component counts as unknown).
- **HA (6.2, 6.4 c)**: one pure function `person_night_rest(rule, lean, temperament, day_tick)` (the rule while the clock lies in the person's own window: bedtime to the unchanged end), used through a `move` closure `rest_for(Option<&Temperament>) -> (night_rest, fatigue_threshold)` that returns the old global pair unchanged when the rule or the component is absent (the old path exactly). Sites: the builder-eligibility query (old L707), `social_eligible_by_pos` (L858), the two peer maps (L913, L930: the peer's own window), and in the main loop the per-person pair **shadows** the outer `night_rest` / `fatigue_threshold` at the top of the body, so every in-loop read is per person: L1051, L1065, L1267, L1270, L1273, L1674, L1697, L1744, L1784 and the sleep branch L2075 (grep confirms these are all in-loop reads). The unfiltered reads (L1852, L2260, L2271, `beds.rs` L23/L92) and the probe's global `fatigue_threshold_at` are unchanged. `Option<&Temperament>` is added to the four queries that already visit every agent; no per-agent map lookup was added.
- **P (6.2, 6.4 c)** — `haul_threshold` reads classified: production per-person sites (workers only: not child, not elder) — decision builder check L699 (food) and L701 (wood), `loaded` L1031 (wood) and L1032 (food), forage `light` L1503, firewood `light` L1528, gather `bounded` L1560, wood pickup amount L2039; movement `hauls_home` L110/L130–131 (through per-agent copies of the forage / firewood rules with the leaned `haul_threshold`, `elder.is_none()`; `child` was already excluded there). Test fixtures, untouched: `firewood.rs` L325, `movement.rs` L398, `agent_decision.rs` L2499. No other read exists (grep over `sim-systems`, `sim-engine`, `sim-bridge`, `sim-core`).
- **NS tether (6.4 b)**: only the adult branch's fallback `evening_radius.or(rules.idle_tether_radius)` — the base is replaced by `tether(base, t)`; the evening radius, children and elders are untouched. **NS departure (6.4 e)**: with effects on, `(departure_key(keyed_unit_f64(seed, STREAM_FISSION, key, tick, 0, rep), t), AgentId)` sorted with `total_cmp` then id; the resulting order is turned back into the old `Vec<(u64, AgentId, bool)>` with the rank as the u64 so the group code below is untouched; a member without the component counts as NS 500 (never happens in v20). With effects off the old block is kept verbatim inside the `else`.
- **Scenes (6.3)**: `scenes::game_v20_scene` / `game_v20_inert_scene` (+ `_with`), `WorldGenConfig::game_v20(_with)` = v19's; probe `args.rs` accepts both (the old `game_v20`-is-unknown assertion now checks `game_v21`), `runner.rs` `scene_engine` and `twin_checked` list them. Not in the recorder (`worldmodel/**` untouched).
- **Probe flag without a new field (6.5, section 2)**: `ProbeArgs` is an existing public struct, so `--agent-trace` lives in a new type `ProbeOptions` (`ProbeOptions::split(argv)` strips the flag before `ProbeArgs::parse`), with a new `probe::run_with(args, options)`; `run(args)` = `run_with(args, &default)`. Likewise `worldsim_perf --rules` (8.5) adds no field to `PerfArgs` / `RunSpec`: for `stress` / `quick` / `map` the choice is kept in the existing `scene` field (which those modes ignored before, so today's default is unchanged) and becomes the run label `stress_v19` / `stress_v20` / `stress_v20_inert`, from which `build_engine` picks the rules (`perf::stress_rules`). `--rules` with `natural` / `overhead` is an error.
- **Observer conventions (6.5)**: the clock minute is the system tick's (`resources.current_tick` after `engine.tick()`) plus the offset; "member of a settlement" = listed in a settlement's `member_agents` (first settlement in id order, as the decision system), hearth = its `formation_tile`; deposits = a drop of carried food (wood) while within `SETTLEMENT_PROXIMITY_RADIUS` (5) of that hearth (the deposit system's own test); a v19 newborn's would-be temperament uses the would-be values of the `AgentBorn` parents that were alive at the end of the previous tick (mirroring the v20 system, which reads living parents' components); in v20 runs the computed value is compared with the component (`component_mismatches`, expected 0). Fissions are written to `_fissions.jsonl` with the eligible set = the parent's living non-elder members at the departure tick ∪ the departers (children are non-elders and are ranked by the system too); `_people.jsonl` holds every person (for T4) and `_trace_meta.json` the misses, mismatches and fission statuses. With the flag on, the existing probe outputs are byte-identical (checked: `game_v19`, 2 seeds × 40 days, summary 0 differences of 25,528 values, per-seed JSONL identical).
- **Bridge (6.6)**: `build_game_engine(seed)` (`game_v20`) is what `init` and `reset_world` call; `reset_world_stress` stays on v19; `get_agent_temperament(entity_bits)` forwards to `collect_agent_temperament`; `get_agent_detail` keeps its nine keys. Nothing in `scripts/` calls the new getter.

- Stage A implementation choices, decided before code/evaluation: the supplied place rank has no tile-varying input, so equal-occupancy candidates share that rank and ties literally use `(y,x)`. No invented tile salt. A new `RoutineObservation` component carries only the most recent decision event (`Started{redrawn}`, `NoPlace{redrawn}`, `Ended{completed}`), with tick/kind; all cumulative counts remain observer-only. A stale activity uses the same one-tick re-entry guard as an interrupted activity, preventing same-tick event overwrite and archetype churn.
- Stage A R2 social rate will use pooled adult completed interactions divided by pooled non-elder adult days, bootstrapping worlds; the mean of world rates is reported as sensitivity. Noon stores are sums across settlements, averaged per world/day. The final table will be committed before either evaluation arm runs.

- R2 shelter count: completed shelters are all newly present complete Shelter buildings, including daughter-camp boot shelters. `building_kinds` has no runtime removal; production insertions are construction completion and new daughter-camp placements. Therefore final minus initial Shelter count is exact for this definition. It will be frozen in the pre-registration and evaluator output, rather than mislabeled as construction work only.
- Routine core verification: sim-core 344 tests passed (including five new formula/boundary tests); sim-core all-target Clippy passed. No stage verdict follows from these code checks; measured tuning/evaluation and identity proofs remain pending.

- Selected activity peers: social memory can select a peer whose own loneliness is not breached, and the social system requires reciprocal consuming states rather than recruiting that peer. To honor same-tick interruption without duplicating the old Idle cascade, actual selections queue invitations. An already-processed activity holder alone is replayed through the same decision body after releasing the query, retaining the tick snapshots, claims and event tags. Every activity is removed at most once and the one-tick re-entry guard prevents cycles. No unconditional new combat exit is introduced. A new `RoutineObservationBatch` preserves multiple same-tick lifecycle events; observers prefer that tick-matching batch over the latest-event component.
- Evaluator convention fixed before tuning/evaluation: each statistic starts `default_rng(0)` and draws 2,000 whole-world resamples. Undefined resample statistics are excluded from percentile intervals, with the valid count reported out of 2,000; an undefined original statistic or no valid resamples is ERROR. R2 social's mean-world-rate sensitivity excludes zero-exposure worlds and reports their count. Missing identity/performance evidence remains PENDING during tuning and ERROR during evaluation; evidence files are SHA256-bound.
- Part 1 integration checks after ordered-event batching: sim-core 344 tests, probe library 87 tests, routine runtime 15 tests, and performance evaluator 20 tests passed. The performance evaluator tests use fake binaries and a temporary lock; they are not measured performance evidence.

- Part 1 retained failures: Clippy initially rejected an explicit nested movement-map type; collection type inference removed that warning without changing data or behavior. A new social regression incorrectly expected its progress map to clear on the completion tick. Existing control and old/new paired runtime tests prove progress 3 remains at tick 604 and is cleared at 605, with one Started at 601 and one Completed at 604. Only the new test expectation changed. LSP status was unavailable because its MCP transport was closed; compiler/Clippy/test evidence is recorded separately. Final metric evaluator has 24 passing tests, including complete ever-adult inventory validation.

- Stage A verdict and I5 baseline were committed as `44aebafa5acf5668d21418f611d8f0ce3a5d13d3` and remote WIP matched that SHA before Part 4 implementation. The green branch remains `adfdecfece79ab019964729319389fd602685fab`. Its measured registration-to-launch interval is 16.982376 seconds, with all 64 held-out trace metadata files bound to the producer and zero miss counters.
- Stage B ownership: coordinator owns core family types, stream, rules and registration; separate executors own runtime pairing/births/households, optional trace and scene wiring, bridge, evaluator, and external runners. No Stage B evaluation seed runs before its own registration. No authorized Stage B feature has been dropped.
- Family close-kin exclusion compares every known parent identity, including a parent appearing in opposite mother/father slots across fallback births; unknown `None` parents never create kinship. This preserves recorded genealogical exclusion despite old-rule fallback slots not implying biological sex.
- Household interpretation: use mutual living couples within the settlement; attach Child-stage children to a recorded parent's couple, mother first if links conflict, assigning each person once in AgentId order. If the father is dead/missing, a child joins the living mother's household even if she is unpaired. No extra father-widower rule is invented. Build the complete household partition before removing elders from fission eligibility.
- Same-tick family observation uses a new `FamilyObservations` component on a non-Agent entity, containing only one tick's ordered `FamilyEvent` list. It survives subject deaths, records exact pairing and newborn identity/fallback, and preserves the parent household/couple snapshot at departure. Fission observations join the existing record by id and only completed fissions enter F5. This adds no field to existing public structs, `Tallies`, `FissionRecord` or `SimResources`.
- Family bridge identity contract: input is hecs entity bits, as in existing detail getters; returned mother/father/partner/children identifiers are stable `AgentId` values, joined through snapshot `agent_ids`. Known parent links survive parent death; `children` contains living children only, sorted by AgentId. The separate activity getter continues using entity bits in its `ids` array.

- Stage B sampling conventions fixed before tuning: F1 at elapsed day 60 (tick 86399) counts a partnership only when the partner is alive and the link is mutual; stale dead-partner pointers do not count. F2 uses one-based elapsed simulation day `tick / 1440 + 1`, so births from day 20 start at tick 27360. Display-clock offset governs hourly/pairing/night observations, not elapsed cohort-day labels. F4 counts each mutual living couple once and requires both assigned beds. F3 retains fallback children with two known equal parent ids as full siblings and excludes any shared known parent identity from unrelated controls.
- Family fission keeps the old stock-transfer calculation based on target `k`, including when a seed household raises actual group size above `k`; only group selection is authorized to change. This can change per-person starting stock in an oversized seed group and is reported rather than silently changing the economy formula. The existing minimum-adult eligibility remains required.

- Part 4 focused integration: core 320 tests, systems 142 tests and bridge 33 tests pass; warning-denied systems/bridge all-target Clippy passes. Two initial Clippy attempts failed on three then one missing public API documentation comments; the required comments were added without lint suppression, and both failed logs are preserved. These checks used the in-progress Part 4 tree, not final cohort producer provenance.
- Actual live-v22 Godot bridge smoke passes 13,563 checks through tick 3660: 286 activity rows join state 4/target 8, a mutual pair (AgentIds 0 and 1) is observed, and newborn 25 links to living parents whose children arrays include it. That first newborn is an early fallback (mother slot 0 is male, father slot 1 female), explicitly preserving the old fallback identity semantics; later real-couple parent selection is checked separately in runtime/trace evidence. No art-rendering acceptance is inferred from this API smoke.
- Family traces represent living unassigned/traveling people with settlement `null`. The evaluator retains those people in living F1/F4 populations and excludes only their unassigned observations from same-settlement F3 pairing; it reports unassigned exposure, and never invents a settlement id.

- `subprofile` (6.1) design: `scope!(name, { block })` is defined twice in `sim-systems/src/lib.rs`; without the feature it expands to `$body`. Scope pairs that would break `let` scoping use `#[cfg(feature = "subprofile")]` statements around a `Timer`. Move counting uses feature-only `world.contains`/`world.get` checks before inserts and the `remove_one` result; the feature-off code differs from Pass 17 only by evaluating the `entities`/`keys` maps and `places::collect` before the `RoutineTick` literal (pure reads) and binding `remove_one`'s result before discarding it. Feature-off proof: Part 1 I1–I6 and all pins equal the base; additionally, adding feature-only scopes later (`c867aaa3`) left the release `worldsim_probe`/`worldsim_perf` byte-identical (`cmp`).

## Pass 19

Run by Claude Code (Opus 5.5) unattended in `/Users/rexxa/work/new-world-pass4`, brief `docs/revitalize/PASS19_GOAL.md` (committed unchanged, `b1e2e2d8`). Base `20a1d07892f7127668a9c2d3c4e649d4a34d352e` on `codex/revitalize-2026-10-wip`, `codex/revitalize-2026-10` at `4ef34b97` (both verified by `git rev-parse` after `git fetch`, 2026-10-16 11:20 KST). No quiet lock existed at start.

- Start checks: `.git/hooks/pre-commit` and `post-commit` absent; `.claude/settings.json` is `S` (skip-worktree) and `{}`; never staged. The local `.gitignore` edit and `.ignore` stay uncommitted.
- Seeds (4.2): `grep -rnE -- '--seeds +19(2|3)[0-9]{2}' docs/revitalize tools/revitalize` and `find docs/revitalize -name '*_s19[23][0-9][0-9][._]*'` were both empty, so evaluation 19200–19231 and smoke 19300–19303 stand.
- Shared-Mac tools (1.2): `wait_mac_p19.sh` / `quiet_lock_p19.sh` are Pass 18's with the owner string `pass19-sim (Claude Code, new-world-pass4)`, `P19_QUIET_*` and the `p19-data` spacing stamp. New `run_watched_p19.sh` runs every build, test, probe batch and gate step in its own process group with a 10 s watcher: `kill -STOP -<pgid>` while a foreign lock exists, `kill -CONT` when it is gone, stopped intervals logged to the RUNLOG and optionally to `WATCH_STATUS` (a timed step that was stopped is re-run). The group id is registered under `p19-data/watched/`. My own quiet hold writes `p19-data/watched/PAUSE` after the foreign-lock wait, so my untimed jobs stop too, waits 90 s for the 1-minute load to settle, and its busy check ignores my registered (stopped) groups; PAUSE is removed between quiet polls and on release. Self-test: a 30 s job was stopped 20.0 s by PAUSE and resumed (`stopped_total=20.0s`). `identity_p19.sh` = `identity_p18.sh` + I7 (`game_v23`), every probe and the recorder watched; modes `all`, `--i567-only`, `--no-i567`.
- Base worktree `../new-world-p19-base` (detached at `20a1d078`) got the main clone's `rust/Cargo.lock` before its first build; `cargo tree -i hecs` = `hecs v0.10.5` in both.
- Part 0 pins (`5663058f`, committed alone before any other Rust change): `harness_revitalize_pass19_frozen.rs`, one `#[test]` per seed 0–4 for `game_v23` (fingerprint and `state_digest` at 1,440 / 40,320 / 86,400 ticks, births, fissions) plus every `game_v23` rule leaf asserted separately (Pass 18's 150 `assert_eq!` and the birth-rule variant check with the family leaves 200 / 1200 / `elder_partner_follows = true`: 151 `assert_eq!`). Values captured in release from unchanged `20a1d078` code by a temporary uncommitted test file (deleted); release 6/6 in 27.5 s; `cargo clippy -p sim-test --all-targets -D warnings` clean. The day-1 values equal Pass 18's v21/v22 day-1 pins (no pairing yet); seed 0 has one completed fission by day 60.

### Part 1 design decisions (the chronicle)

- Branching: Part 1 and Part 2 were coded on local branches `p19-part1` / `p19-part2` while the base identity ran; nothing is pushed before Part 0's identity record. They are fast-forwarded onto the WIP branch in order (no history rewrite of anything pushed).
- Formation and membership (6.2) are recorded by a **diff of the tick's actual roster changes** rather than by snapshotting every roster before `sync_membership`: `sync_membership` and the formation scan note every (agent, settlement) pair they actually added (`HashSet::insert` returned true), `resolve_exclusivity` every pair it actually removed (`remove_member_agent` returned true). A pair in both is unchanged (the non-fission re-admit/drop cycle the brief warns about, recorded nowhere), so the net joins and leaves are exactly the before/after roster difference for the rest of the tick's roster writes, which happen in later steps (births, recorded as `Born`) or later systems (`Settled`, remnant merge, death). Reason: a full before/after snapshot costs an allocation and a sort of every member every tick (≈ 500 members in the stress camp), which alone could exceed C6's ±3 %; this costs nothing on the usual tick (both lists empty, early return). Same output; proved by the harness test `harness_revitalize_pass19_chronicle.rs`, which rebuilds every living agent's roster from the chronicle's membership entries at every day boundary and compares it with the actual rosters (`game_v23` seed 0 through its fission to day 60, `game_v16` seed 3 without fission, the legacy scene).
- A member of a newly formed settlement that also lost an older roster in the same tick gets `LeftSettlement` cause 2 for the old roster before its `FoundingMember`/`Joined` entry, so the membership history stays consistent (the brief's (2) lists only agents outside new settlements).
- `Joined` cause 1 for an agent that already has a `FoundingMember` entry needs one chronicle read (`Chronicle::has_founding_member`); it chooses which entry is written and nothing else. Recorded as the only chronicle read besides the person-table lookups.
- `run_formation_scan`, `sync_membership`, `resolve_exclusivity` (private) gained a `&mut Vec` parameter; `FissionSystem::remnants` (private) gained `&World` for the person-record lookups. `despawn_agent` iterates `settlements.iter_mut()` instead of `values_mut()` (same mutations) to remember the roster the agent left.
- `PairingSystem` keeps the chosen father's familiarity from the same `min_by` (it previously mapped to the id only); the choice is unchanged.
- `select_departure` keeps its signature and result and is now used only by tests (`#[cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))]`); production calls `select_departure_explained` once. A frozen verbatim copy of Pass 18's function lives in the test module; the property test compares key sets over 2,000 random settlements (random sizes, couples incl. elder partners, children, `group_min`/`group_max`/`min_adults`/`k`, partners `None` / empty / the couples) from a fixed xorshift seed.
- `--chronicle-out` is parsed by a new `probe::chronicle_out::split` and passed to a new `run_with_chronicle` (`run_with` calls it with `None`), so `ProbeOptions` (a `Copy` struct) gained no field. The twin check compares the two chronicles every tick (length, the newly appended entries, and the person tables when entries were appended) and in full at the end of the run.
- Person-table `born_tick` comes from `Life` (absent without an aging rule, as the brief specifies); founders' records are created in `worldgen::camp` after their components are inserted (every scene).

### Part 2 design decisions (`game_v24`)

- Readers of `day_end_minute` (7.3, `grep -rn day_end_minute rust/crates`): production code has exactly two, `RoutineTick::can_enter` (`decision/routine/mod.rs`) and `RoutineTick::deadline` (`decision/routine/lifecycle.rs`); both now call `RoutineRule::end_minute(key, temperament)`. The other hits are the rule literal (`rules.rs`), the field declaration and the Pass 18/19 pin asserts. `can_enter` gained `temperament`; `deadline` gained `key` and `temperament` (both `pub(super)`/`pub(in decision)`; three call sites in `agent_decision.rs`, all with `lineage` and `temperament` in scope). Without a lineage key `deadline` uses the id as the key, as the rest draw already does (`end`); only matters under a stagger and only for someone who entered without a key, which `can_enter` forbids.
- `RoutineRule::end_stagger` carries `#[serde(default)]` so a serialized rule from before Pass 19 still deserializes (as `None`).
- Pre-registration smoke (no tuning; `EndStagger` is the brief's): smoke seeds 19300–19303, 5 days, both rule sets, `bin_p2` (`3322601f`). `game_v24`'s non-interrupted day-end exits spread over 17:00–19:00 (30-minute bins 17:00 3, 17:30 65, 18:00 56, 18:30 5; `game_v23`: all 151 at 18:00) and ρ(P, mean exit minute) = 0.79 over 36 people. But the largest per-tick count of all ends in 16:00–20:00 fell only from a median of 17 to 10.5 per world-day: the largest ticks of `game_v24` are 16:01 (10–19 *interrupted* ends, also present in `game_v23` at 16:01 with 16–19) and 18:01 (9–11 interrupted ends of people still in an activity at 18:00). H1 as the brief defines it counts every end, so it will probably miss its 0.15 bar. The rule is the brief's and is registered unchanged; METRICS.md registers diagnostics beside it (day-end exits only, interrupted only, clock minute of the largest tick). The other 18:00 windows are not touched (7.4).

### Shared-Mac record and measurement holds

- 12:00:20 KST: first request for the combined hold (C6, H5, Part 3 scale). Its settle step (90 s fixed, then release PAUSE during the 5-minute quiet wait) was wrong: my own paused jobs had pushed the 1-minute load to ~60, which needs several minutes to decay, and releasing PAUSE during the wait would let them push it up again (load 24.3 at 12:01:50). I cancelled the request at 12:02 before any lock was taken (no lock, PAUSE released by the cleanup) and changed `quiet_lock_p19.sh` to keep my jobs paused and poll every 15 s for up to 10 minutes until the 1-minute load is < 4 (or foreign work appears in the busy check), only then releasing PAUSE for the 5-minute quiet wait. Re-queued at 12:02:19.
- Edge diagnosis (temporary uncommitted test, deleted; smoke seeds 19300–19303, 5 days, Part 2 code): of the people whose activity ended without completion on the 16:01 tick, 214 (`game_v24`) / 219 (`game_v23`) became `Seeking { GatherFood }` (a few Food/Water); on `game_v24`'s 18:01 tick 53 became `Seeking { GatherFood }`, 12 Idle, 1 Food. So the two other edges are food-gathering trips becoming available to many activity holders at once (the exit test's `gather()` predicate), not the day-end rule; they are outside this pass's scope (7.4: other windows stay where they are). The trace tick of a routine row is the system tick (`game_v23`'s 18:00 edge appears at system minute 1080).
- First rebase (13:05 KST, after the Part 0 push `64fa3831`; nothing of it had been pushed; `git diff` of `rust/` before/after = 0 lines; author times kept): 0ca95174 → 9d6fb0dd (chronicle), 3322601f → bf47f7c6 (`game_v24`), ee6b30c5 → d5939234 (METRICS registration, author time 11:51:15 KST, before the first `game_v24` evaluation probe started at 12:43:47), c2309a70 → a691f989 (perf output). The measurement binaries were built from the pre-rebase commits (`bin_p1` = 0ca95174, `bin_p2` = 3322601f, `bin_p3` = c2309a70), i.e. the same trees; their `WORLDSIM_GIT_SHA` labels carry the pre-rebase short hashes.
- Part 1 checks (6.6): Part 1 identity (`bin_p1`, 11:42–13:16 KST) I1–I7 zero non-timing differences from the base, I2 equal, DETERMINISM PASS ×6 ([validation](probe/p19-1_identity/validation.txt)). C-rules on `game_v23` 19200–19231 (run A 11:51–12:23, run B 12:23–12:53, Part 1 build): C1 0 mismatches, C2 0, C3 0, C4 32/32 files byte-identical; C5 0.096 entries and 12.2 B (memory estimate) / 6.7 B (JSON) per person-day ([chronicle](probe/p19-1_chronicle/chronicle.md)). C6 in the 12:39 hold: mean +2.40 %, p99.9 +1.16 % ([c6](perf/p19-1/c6.md)). C5's projection uses a game year of 28 days (four seasons of seven, `SeasonRule::days_per_season`).
- Quiet hold record: one hold, 12:39:39–12:40:55 KST (76.1 s), load 3.84, uncontended, after waiting 12:02–12:39 (the art run built with cargo at 12:04, 12:15, 12:21 and ran Godot at 12:10, 12:26, 12:31; I took no lock while they worked). Inside it: C6, H5 and the Part 3 scale runs (`perf/p19-{1,2,3}/`). My untimed jobs were SIGSTOPped by PAUSE at every settle check of the wait (12:00, 12:02, 12:09, 12:15, 12:20, 12:26, 12:31) and through the hold (Part 1 I6, base I6 and run B, 12:36:39–12:41:04, 250–260 s): 24 pauses, 1,800 s in total; every watcher STOP was my own PAUSE, none a foreign lock (`p19-data/*/RUNLOG.txt`). No subagent was running during the hold. (Corrected after review round 1, finding 9: the first version named only two of the three stopped groups.)

- Part 2 evaluation (7.6): `game_v24` on 19200–19231, 120 days, Part 2 build (`bin_p2` = 3322601f, published b0c31465), 12:43:47–13:06:42 KST, DETERMINISM PASS; the `game_v23` side is 6.6's run A. One process per (size, repeat), three repeats; `/usr/bin/time -l`'s maximum RSS is flat (26.5 MB) at every size because mapped file pages dominate it, so SCALE.md also reports macOS's "peak memory footprint" from the same output (8.4 / 11.4 / 16.5 MB). Every stress world ended as one settlement of N members (500 / 1,000 / 2,000), as the brief warns. S1 PASS, S2 PASS (0.856 ms), S3 PASS (5.1 % of a core at 2× with 1,000), S4 reported ([SCALE.md](SCALE.md)). An untimed smoke of the scale procedure (1 day, 1 repeat, contended) ran at 11:57 only to validate the scripts; its numbers are not reported.
